# Tray App Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** A tray app, `mailtriage-tray`, for macOS and Linux. It shows at a glance whether mailtriage runs for each account, starts, stops and installs the background service, opens logs, edits categories in a window with a refile panel, and starts at login. Every action is one documented `mailtriage … --json` command, so an agent can do the same with the CLI.

**Architecture:** A new crate `tray/` (package and binary `mailtriage-tray`) joins a Cargo workspace whose root stays the `mailtriage` package. The tray is a thin client of the CLI. `cli` runs one command with a time limit and reads its JSON. `model` holds all logic and is pure: health, the menu, the window's draft, revisions and commands, and every sentence. `tray` (tray-icon on a tao event loop) and `editor` (eframe/egui with the glow renderer and AccessKit) only draw `model` and send actions back. The `mailtriage` crate gains two new modules. `src/categories.rs` holds the categories digest, `changes` and `--expect-digest`. `src/service_control.rs` holds `service status` for every account with the new fields, plus `service start`/`stop` with the service lock. It gets no GUI dependency. Phase B adds the heartbeat `reason` (schema v8), the updater's `mailtriage-tray` component and the release workflow.

**Tech Stack:** Rust 2021 (stable; 1.92 locally), clap 4, serde/serde_json, chrono, fs2, sha2 0.10. The tray crate adds tray-icon 0.26 (muda 0.21), tao 0.37, eframe 0.35 (glow, accesskit), arboard 3, png 0.18, libc 0.2, tempfile 3, and objc2-app-kit 0.3 on macOS. Its tests use egui_kittest 0.35 (eframe, snapshot, wgpu) and image 0.25.

**Spec:** `design/specs/2026-10-06-tray-design.md` is the binding authority; read it before every task. It builds on `2026-10-06-filing-refile-design.md` (the `filing refile` JSON, including `folders`) and `2026-10-06-auto-update-design.md` (the `update` block, restart rule, cache, install steps). `design/plans/2026-10-06-parallel-development.md` binds this plan. Where this plan and the spec disagree, the spec wins, except for the rulings under "Decisions this plan adds".

## How this plan was checked

Every Phase A code block below was compiled and run in a scratch copy of `main` at `ed79779` on macOS arm64 with Rust 1.92: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace --locked` (all green, repeated three times for the window tests). The Linux-only branches (systemd against `/proc`, the `.desktop` file through the binary, GTK) were not run locally; CI runs them. Phase B is written against the specs, because the refile and updater code is not on this branch yet; its heartbeat part was also prototyped on today's schema.

The Phase A diffs and files below were also extracted from this document and applied in order to a fresh copy of `main`. The result was byte-identical to the tested copy and passed all three gates. In one of five full workspace runs, the existing test `tests/key_command.rs::failures_report_fixed_messages_without_output` failed under the heavier parallel load (its key command has a 10 s deadline); it passed on every rerun. It predates this plan. If it flakes in a task's full check, rerun that test before suspecting the task.

## Phase A and Phase B

- This plan runs on branch `feature/tray` in `.worktrees/tray`, created from the shared `main` commit (parallel-development plan). Merge order into `main` is refile, then auto-update, then tray; the controller merges.
- **Phase A** is Tasks 1–9. It needs nothing from the other branches. Cross-feature JSON (refile output with `folders`; the `update` block, `last_pass.version`, `last_pass.reason`) comes from fixture files read by a fake `mailtriage` script. The SDD run executes Phase A, then stops and reports.
- **Phase B** is Tasks 10–11, headed "Phase B (after refile and auto-update are merged into this branch)". The controller first merges `feature/refile` and `feature/auto-update` into this branch, then resumes the run.
- Schema numbers are never renumbered: refile is v6, auto-update v7, the tray's heartbeat `reason` is **v8**.

## Verified toolkit facts

Checked on 2026-10-06 with `cargo search`, `cargo info` and a compiled prototype.

- **Versions.** eframe, egui and egui_kittest 0.36 need Rust 1.95; 0.35 needs 1.92, the local toolchain. This plan pins **0.35**. tray-icon 0.26.0 (MSRV 1.90) uses muda 0.21; tao 0.37.1 (MSRV 1.85). `cargo add` resolved them without changing any dependency of the `mailtriage` package (`cargo tree -p mailtriage -e normal,build` is identical before and after).
- **macOS** builds the tray with nothing installed. A real run on macOS showed the menu bar icon, ran `--version` and `service status --json --config <absolute>`, and refused a second tray.
- **Linux CI** needs the GTK 3 and AppIndicator development packages. muda 0.21 no longer links `libxdo` (tray-icon 0.26 documents GTK 3 and `libappindicator3`/`libayatana-appindicator3` only). CI installs `libxdo-dev` anyway, as the spec asks; it is harmless. `cargo build` at the workspace root builds only the root package, so `e2e.yml`'s `cargo build --locked` stays free of GUI libraries.
- **tao 0.37:** `EventLoopBuilder::<T>::with_user_event().build()`, `EventLoopExtMacOS::set_activation_policy(ActivationPolicy::Accessory)`, `Event::NewEvents(StartCause::Init)`, `ControlFlow::WaitUntil`. `EventLoop::run` never returns.
- **tray-icon 0.26:** `with_icon_as_template` and `set_icon_with_as_template` are deprecated (clippy `-D warnings` fails on them). Use `TrayIcon::set_icon_templated` (macOS only) and `set_icon`. `MenuEvent::set_event_handler(Some(f))` takes `f: Fn(MenuEvent) + Send + Sync`; `MenuEvent.id.0` is the item id.
- **eframe 0.35:** the app implements `App::ui(&mut self, ui: &mut egui::Ui, frame: &mut eframe::Frame)`; there is no `update`. Panels are `egui::Panel::{top,bottom,left}(id).show(ui, …)` and `egui::CentralPanel::default().show(ui, …)`; `show_inside` is deprecated. A dialog is `egui::Modal::new(id).show(ctx, …)` with `should_close()` for Escape and backdrop clicks. `Button::selectable(selected, text)` draws a list row.
- **egui_kittest 0.35:**
  - `Harness::builder().with_size(..).with_theme(..).wgpu().build_eframe(|cc| app)` builds the harness; `render()` needs the `wgpu` feature and works headless on macOS (Metal).
  - Queries: `get_by_label`, `query_all_by_label_contains`. Node methods: `click`, `focus`, `type_text`, `value`, and `accesskit_node().is_disabled()`.
  - A text field is found by its label only when drawn with `.labelled_by(label.id)`; hint text is no label.
  - egui lays a newly shown dialog out over two frames, so a test must step twice more before clicking its buttons (the `click` helper).
  - Sending AccessKit `scroll_to_me` right before a click made the click miss; the helpers never do that.
- **Real `launchctl`** (read-only probes): `print` shows the arguments as a block, `\targuments = {`, `\t\t<arg>`, `\t}`. `print` of an unknown job exits 113. `print-disabled gui/<uid>` lists `\t\t"label" => enabled|disabled`.
- **Screenshots:** the menu bar cannot be captured here (`screencapture` fails without Screen Recording permission). Window screens render offscreen with wgpu, and menus are printed as text; the real menu bar is a human check.

## Decisions this plan adds

Rulings on points the spec leaves open. Each says what it costs if wrong.

1. **Toolkit versions:** eframe/egui/egui_kittest 0.35, tray-icon 0.26, tao 0.37, so the workspace builds with the local Rust 1.92. Cost: a version bump later.
2. **No dependency on the `mailtriage` crate** from the tray: no rusqlite or reqwest in the tray binary. It has its own process runner (`tray/src/cli.rs`). Cost: about 80 duplicated lines.
3. **Versions:** `tray/Cargo.toml` states the version itself and must equal the root's. A tray test and CI's version step check it. Cost: one line per release (`docs/development/releases.md` says so).
4. **Argument order:** `--config <absolute path>` goes **last** in every `mailtriage` command, for example `service start --account A --json --config /abs/mailtriage.json`; windows start as `mailtriage-tray categories --account A --config C --mailtriage M`. `mailtriage --version` runs without `--config`. Cost: the parity test's expected lines.
5. **Digest:** `filing_on` is the configured `filing.mode != off`, the rule `apply`'s folder normalization uses, not the effective mode of an account without an engine. `categories validate --account` reports the **account's current** digest (what `--expect-digest` compares), not one of the file. `apply`'s order is: config lock, unchanged-file guard, digest, then everything else. Cost: one function.
6. **`changes`:** added, renamed, re-pointed and edited categories are listed in the file's order, removed ones in the current order. `folders_changed` compares effective folders in every filing mode (a rename with filing off changes the effective folder too); the window leaves folder changes out of its words while filing is `off`. Cost: one condition.
7. **`service status`'s `filing_mode`** is the configured mode. Cost: none for the tray.
8. **Establishing `service_config`:**

   | Manager and job | `service_config` | Configs compared for `config_matches` |
   | --- | --- | --- |
   | launchd, `print` exits 0 | `arguments` block | it, file |
   | launchd, `print` exits 113 (not loaded) | unit file | file |
   | launchd, any other failure | `null` | `null`, file |
   | systemd, loaded and running | `/proc/<MainPID>/cmdline`, start time unchanged | it, loaded `ExecStart`, file |
   | systemd, loaded, not running | loaded `ExecStart` | it, file |
   | systemd, not loaded | unit file | file |
   | systemd, `show` fails | `null` | `null`, file |

   - A config that differs gives `false`. Otherwise any `null` gives `null`, and so does systemd's `NeedDaemonReload=yes` (or unknown); then `true`. The spec leaves the case of matching configs with a needed reload open; this plan makes it `null`.
   - `ExecStart` from `systemctl show` counts only in the shape `service install` writes: 11 words, `watch`, `--config`, `--account`, `--interval-seconds`. A path that `show` printed unquoted therefore gives `null`, never a wrong path.
   - `/proc` and plist arguments are exact; there `--config`'s value is taken wherever it stands.
   - `needs_daemon_reload` appears only for systemd.

   Cost: one table.
9. **`enablement`** is `unknown` when the query fails, for both managers. Cost: one string.
10. **`service stop` when not installed** exits 2 with `start`'s message. `stop` reports `stopped` when launchd had the job loaded, or systemd had it running, before; otherwise `already_stopped`. Cost: one branch.
11. **Service lock:**
    - Its directory follows the manager: launchd uses `~/Library/Caches/mailtriage`, systemd `~/.cache/mailtriage`, always from `HOME`.
    - The account name is checked before the lock, because it becomes part of a file name.
    - `setup`'s step 10 takes the same lock around its install.
    - The lock wait (30 s) and `start`'s wait (5 s) are parameters, so tests can shorten them.

    Cost: one line in setup.
12. **Messages:**
    - mismatch: `service for account A runs config X; pass --config X` (shell-quoted);
    - unknown: `cannot tell which config the service for account A runs; try again, or reinstall it with mailtriage service install --account A`;
    - busy: `another service command for account A is running; try again`;
    - not started: `service did not start; see LOG`, where LOG is the `.err` file on launchd and `journalctl --user -u mailtriage-A.service` on systemd.

    Cost: strings.
13. **"No config":** the tray recognises it when `--config` names a missing file, or when the first `service status` fails with code 2 and a message starting `configuration not found`; no `reason` exists for it. Cost: one prefix match.
14. **Fields the tray needs.** A missing one gives "mailtriage is older than the tray; run `mailtriage update`." with the missing field in "Show details".
    - Top level: `config` and `services`.
    - Each service: `account`, `manager`, `installed`, `running`, `pid`, `log_paths`, `last_pass`, `config_matches`, `enabled`, `enablement`, `interval_seconds`, `filing_mode`, `identity`.
    - Optional: `update`, `last_pass.reason`, `last_pass.version`, `service_config`, `file_config`, `needs_daemon_reload`, `unit_path`.

    Cost: a list.
15. **Health details:**
    - Any last-pass exit code other than 0, 4, and 5 with `config_changed` or `account_busy` is an error. Heartbeats only record 0, 2, 3, 4 and 5.
    - An `interval_seconds` of `null` counts as 60.
    - "Problem since" is the time the tray first saw the job not running, or the failed pass's time.
    - The summary also reads "N of M accounts running" when running and stopped accounts mix (`restarting` counts as running), and "No background service on this system" when every account is `unavailable`.
    - Row marks: `●` running, `○` off, `▲` attention.

    Cost: words.
16. **Menu:**
    - "Open log" for every installed launchd service, "Copy log command" for systemd, "Open error log" for a launchd `error`.
    - While a service action runs, its item reads "Starting…", "Stopping…" or "Installing…"; menus cannot show spinners.
    - Action failures and window notices appear under the summary for 60 s; "Show details" copies the details.
    - The clipboard is `arboard`, kept alive for the tray's lifetime (X11 needs a living owner).

    Cost: small.
17. **CLI version for the mismatch line:** `update.installed` of a service whose `update.executable` is the tray's CLI path, else `mailtriage --version`. The tray runs `--version` again only when the CLI file's identity changes. Cost: one probe.
18. **Tray timing:**
    - It refreshes every 15 s, and reaps windows and checks for a restart at each refresh.
    - It re-executes only when the paths are resolved and no tray command runs; until the paths are resolved it never does.

    Cost: none.
19. **Window:**
    - Every load runs `service status` (accounts and filing modes), then `categories export`.
    - Revisions only grow. A re-check of the same revision (after `config_changed`) reuses its draft file, which is never rewritten. The draft files of ignored answers and superseded checks are deleted.
    - "Saved." survives the reload after a save.
    - Closing waits only for writes (apply, refile), not for reads.
    - The first load's `status` or `export` failing gives exit 3 (the spec names `export`).
    - The default category cannot be removed: the button is disabled with "Choose another default category first".
    - Revert does not ask (it touches unsaved edits only).
    - Add creates "New category" (then "New category 2", …).
    - The folder field shows the effective folder for existing categories with filing on; with filing off it shows the stored folder or stays empty. It is trimmed when serialized.

    Cost: small each.
20. **Refile panel:**
    - It is a fixed 232 px panel below the form, above the footer. After a move the window runs `service status` (for the "not running" line, using `model::health` over its own observations) and the preview again.
    - A retired folder's button reads "Move"; its accessible label is "Move mail from <folder>", so screen readers and tests can tell them apart.
    - With nothing to move it says "No filed mail needs moving.".
    - Skipped reasons in words: `not_filed_by_mailtriage` "not filed by mailtriage", `corrected` "corrected by you", `pinned` "kept in place by you", `blocked` "held back after a problem", `done` "marked done", `open_intent` "already being moved", `explicit_target` "waiting for a move you asked for", `multiple_copies` "in more than one folder", `incomplete_input` "not fully read", `retired_frozen` "in a folder no longer checked", `target_unusable` "their new folder is not ready", `target_inbox_or_source` "would go back to the inbox". Unknown reasons use their words with spaces.
    - The refile plan fixes `waiting` as a count and `waiting_marked` as always present; the tray defaults `waiting_marked` to 0 anyway.

    Cost: words.
21. **Plain words for CLI errors:**
    - `invalid categories…` becomes "Every category needs a name, a description and its own ID, and exactly one must be the default category.";
    - `categories need a valid folder: ids` names the categories;
    - `category has manual corrections…` becomes "You corrected some mail to a category you removed. Correct that mail to another category first.";
    - anything else shows as it is, with "Show details".

    Cost: three strings.
22. **Second window:** it prints `the categories window is already open` on both platforms, after bringing the window to the front on macOS. The tray reads its child's stdout and shows the line as a notice. Cost: one line of output on macOS.
23. **Autostart:**
    - The `.desktop` marker is a first line `# managed by mailtriage`.
    - `status` of an unmarked file exits 5 too.
    - A failed `print-disabled` counts as not disabled (launchd's default).
    - `enable` also resolves the CLI; a missing one exits 3 and names `--mailtriage`.
    - The menu item calls the same functions in-process.
    - Without `--json` the result prints as pretty JSON, like mailtriage.

    Cost: small.
24. **Icons:** 36×36 PNGs drawn by `tray/examples/make_icons.rs` and committed. The shape is an envelope: plain for ok, with a `!` badge for warning and error, as an outline for off. macOS uses black templates, so warning and error share the badged shape. Linux uses green, amber, red and grey. Cost: redraw with the example.
25. **Agent parity:** the fake CLI does not read `MAILTRIAGE_CONFIG`. The "config only in `MAILTRIAGE_CONFIG`" case is therefore a tray started without `--config`, whose first `service status` (without `--config`) reports `config`; every later command must carry it. Cost: none.
26. **Design review:** window screens render offscreen in light and dark mode (ignored kittest tests), and menus are printed as text for every state. The real menu bar, VoiceOver and Orca are human checks in `docs/development/verification.md`. Cost: a person runs them.
27. **Phase B, heartbeat:** `sync` also records an `account_busy` heartbeat when another worker holds the account's lock. Until now only passes that held the lock recorded one, but the tray spec needs `account_busy` (exit 5, warning). Accepted race: the holder's own heartbeat at the end of its pass overwrites it. Cost: one branch.
28. **Phase B, update:**
    - Exit 4 comes from a top-level `"partial": true` in `update`'s result, which the CLI already maps to 4.
    - A skipped tray still writes its `installs` entry, with `last_error` and without backoff (`failures` unchanged).
    - A `Component` enum names the two programs.

    Cost: one field.

## Global Constraints

- Work on `feature/tray` in `.worktrees/tray`. Commit at the end of every task. Never push or merge. Every commit message ends with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`.
- After every task, all three pass at the workspace root: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace --locked`. CI runs them on Linux amd64, Linux arm64 and macOS arm64, so tests must not depend on the host platform unless they are `#[cfg]`-gated.
- The `mailtriage` package gains no dependency. Its `Cargo.toml` changes only by the `[workspace]` table (Task 4), and `cargo tree -p mailtriage -e normal,build` stays identical. The `mailtriage` binary gets no GUI dependency.
- Tests never run the real `launchctl`, `systemctl` or keychain, and never write into the real `HOME`, cache, LaunchAgents, autostart directory or `~/.cargo/bin`. They never open a real window or menu bar: kittest is headless, and tray and window processes started by tests exit before any GUI.
- The OpenRouter key is never in a file, fixture or doc recipe.
- Edits to shared files (`src/cli.rs`, `src/service.rs`, `src/system_service.rs`, `src/store.rs`, `docs/guide.md`, `docs/agents/index.md`, `README.md`, `.github/workflows/*`, `Cargo.toml`) stay small and additive. New code goes into new modules (`src/categories.rs`, `src/service_control.rs`, `tray/`), and docs into new sections.
- JSON keeps `schema_version: 1`. The error object is `{"code","message"}` plus `"reason"` only when set. New reasons, all with code 5: `categories_changed`, `service_config_mismatch`, `service_config_unknown`, `service_busy`.
- Exit codes are verbatim from the spec:
  - `service start`/`stop`: 2 for an unknown account, an unsupported platform or (start) a service that is not installed; 3 when `launchctl`/`systemctl` fails or the job did not start; 5 with the reasons above.
  - `mailtriage-tray categories`: 0, 2, 3.
  - `mailtriage-tray autostart`: 0, 2, 3, 5.
- Paths:
  - The CLI is `--mailtriage`, else `mailtriage` next to the tray's canonical path, else the first on `PATH`.
  - The config is `--config`, else the `config` of the first `service status`.
  - Both are canonicalized once and passed explicitly to every command, window and login item.
- Times:

  | What | Value |
  | --- | --- |
  | `service status` limit | 30 s |
  | Other commands' limit | 120 s |
  | Check delay after an edit | 500 ms |
  | `config_busy` retries | 3, 2 s apart |
  | Stale menu kept | 2 min |
  | Restart grace | 2 min |
  | Staleness | 3 × interval + 30 min |
  | Tray refresh | 15 s |
  | Service lock wait | 30 s |
  | `start` wait | 5 s |

- Locks:
  - `tray.lock`, and `editor-<16 hex>.lock` with its `.pid`, live in the update spec's cache directory (`~/Library/Caches/mailtriage`; `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/mailtriage` when absolute, else `~/.cache/mailtriage`).
  - `service-<label>.lock` lives in the HOME-derived directory and ignores `XDG_CACHE_HOME`.
- UI principles, verbatim from the spec:
  - plain words;
  - a calm layout, with one primary button (Apply) bottom right and Revert beside it;
  - destructive actions ask first and say what happens to mail;
  - the look follows the system's light or dark mode;
  - spacing on an 8 px grid, text 14 pt, headings 18 pt;
  - every command shows a spinner and a verb;
  - success says what happens next, and disabled controls say why;
  - empty states explain what to do;
  - keyboard: ⌘/Ctrl+N adds a category, ⌘/Ctrl+S opens the apply confirmation, Esc closes dialogs, ⌘/Ctrl+W closes the window, Tab follows the visual order;
  - AccessKit is on, with an accessible label for every control.
- The classification generation hash (`6dfd7bf30e6bf1c0b27ee97af991ecf21dc5ac0400044c2f3adbda7f79d37514`) and the binding identity golden value (`c4a294832a82e8e274fb8bcdf9c2c0daa895f811efe13776ab60da3c7fd50aaa`) do not change.
- Existing tests keep passing unchanged, with these permitted edits:
  - `tests/common/mod.rs`'s fake `launchctl`/`systemctl` grow (Task 2);
  - `tests/system_service.rs`'s expected launchd call lists gain `enable`, and its `SLOW_LAUNCHCTL` accepts `enable` (Task 3);
  - assertions that pin the latest schema version move to 8 (Task 10).

## Review Focus

These are the inputs most likely to hurt a person that no task's main tests aim at. Each has a pinned test.

1. **Paths with spaces and special characters**, such as `/Users/a & b/50% $HOME "x"/mailtriage.json` or `/opt/mail triage/mailtriage`. They pass through unit files, `launchctl print`, `systemctl show`, `/proc`, the plist and `.desktop` login items, and every tray command, and must stay one argument everywhere. Tests:
   - Task 2: `unit_and_plist_arguments_invert_their_writers`, `exec_start_needs_the_install_shape`;
   - Task 4: `every_request_is_its_documented_command_with_the_config_last`;
   - Task 6: `the_plist_records_both_paths`, `the_desktop_entry_quotes_exec_per_the_specification`.
2. **Two configs that share an account name.** A service is named per account, not per config. Status must not claim another config's job, and start and stop must refuse it. Tests:
   - Task 2: `two_configs_that_share_an_account_name`, `a_unit_rewritten_for_another_config_while_the_old_job_runs`, `systemd_compares_the_running_process_too`;
   - Task 3: `another_or_unknown_config_is_refused_without_manager_calls`.
3. **Overlapping answers and processes:** answers for older revisions or other accounts, superseded loads, concurrent CLI calls whose exit status the tray must not steal, and the fake's log under concurrency. Tests:
   - Task 5: the window model tests;
   - Task 7: `only_tracked_windows_are_reaped`;
   - Task 8: `a_validation_error_shows_inline_and_an_older_answer_does_not_enable_apply`. The fake CLI writes one line per call, so the parity test counts reliably.
4. **A CLI older or newer than the tray.** Missing fields say "update mailtriage", extra fields are ignored, and Phase-B fields are optional. Tests:
   - Task 4: `a_cli_without_the_new_fields_asks_for_an_update`, `status_is_read_with_the_fields_of_later_releases`.
5. **Edits that end where they started:** typing a value back, Revert, an account switch with edits, the reload after a save. Apply must be enabled exactly when the latest finished check is for the current revision and the draft differs from what was loaded. Tests:
   - Task 5: `apply_explains_why_it_is_disabled`, `superseded_loads_are_ignored_and_switching_with_edits_asks_first`, `apply_confirms_then_runs_with_the_load_digest_and_reloads` (the reload keeps "Saved.").

## Phase A

### Task 1: Category digest, `--expect-digest` and `changes`

**Files:**
- Create: `src/categories.rs`, `tests/categories_digest.rs`
- Modify:
  - `src/lib.rs` (module);
  - `src/service.rs`: `ErrorKind`, `categories`, `apply_categories`, `validate_categories`, `categories_candidate`, `category_semantics`, and the `error_reasons` test;
  - `src/cli.rs`: `CategoriesCommand::Apply`;
  - `docs/guide.md`, `docs/agents/index.md`, `docs/development/service-api.md`.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `domain::{AccountConfig, Category, FilingMode}`; `service::category_semantics` (made `pub(crate)`), the rule `categories_candidate` uses to advance `taxonomy_revision`.
- Produces:
  - `categories::filing_on(&AccountConfig) -> bool`
  - `categories::digest(filing_on: bool, categories: &[Category]) -> String`, which returns `v1:` and 64 lowercase hex digits
  - `categories::account_digest(&AccountConfig) -> String`
  - `categories::normalized(previous: &AccountConfig, categories: Vec<Category>) -> Vec<Category>`, apply's folder normalization moved out of `categories_candidate`
  - `categories::changes(previous: &[Category], next: &[Category]) -> Changes`
  - `Changes { added: Vec<String>, removed: Vec<Removed>, renamed: Vec<Change>, folders_changed: Vec<Change>, edited: Vec<String>, reclassifies: bool }`, `Removed { id, folder }` and `Change { id, from, to }`, all `Serialize`
  - `Service::apply_categories_expecting(&mut self, name: &str, categories: Vec<Category>, expected: Option<&str>) -> Result<Value>`; `apply_categories` calls it with `None`
  - `ErrorKind::CategoriesChanged`, whose reason is `"categories_changed"`
  - JSON:
    - `categories export` gains `digest`;
    - `categories validate --account` gains `digest` (the account's current one) and `changes`;
    - `categories apply` returns export's shape, so it gains `digest` too;
    - `categories apply --expect-digest D`.

The digest test vectors were computed with this exact code; do not change them.

Create `tests/categories_digest.rs`:

```rust
//! The categories digest, `categories apply --expect-digest` and the
//! `changes` of `categories validate --account`.
use mailtriage::{
    categories::{self, Changes},
    config,
    domain::{AppConfig, Category, FilingMode},
    service::{ErrorKind, Service, ServiceError},
};
use serde_json::{json, Value};
use std::{
    fs,
    path::{Path, PathBuf},
    process::Command,
};

const DEFAULT_OFF: &str = "v1:fe333646885134da461c3ef57080b76e331c276b50c3e1fa44935bdd418f02ac";
const DEFAULT_ON: &str = "v1:c0fe803aca4c84827ee579f2d8ba4e1ce496e3cd9f0fc493a034cf030dceee69";
const UNICODE_ON: &str = "v1:7733c397ba1df0b0d3ffe42025d7551f2f164d2413ce93c4ef65c1a60b1591f5";

fn defaults() -> Vec<Category> {
    config::default_config().accounts["work"].categories.clone()
}

fn parse(text: &str) -> Vec<Category> {
    serde_json::from_str(text).unwrap()
}

#[test]
fn digests_are_fixed_vectors() {
    assert_eq!(categories::digest(false, &defaults()), DEFAULT_OFF);
    assert_eq!(categories::digest(true, &defaults()), DEFAULT_ON);
    let mut swapped = defaults();
    swapped.swap(0, 1);
    assert_ne!(categories::digest(false, &swapped), DEFAULT_OFF);
    let unicode = parse(
        r#"[{"id":"cafe","name":"Café ☕ Rechnungen","description":"Belege für Kaffee","catch_all":true,"folder":"Cafe"}]"#,
    );
    assert_eq!(categories::digest(true, &unicode), UNICODE_ON);
    let reordered = parse(
        r#"[{"folder":"Cafe","catch_all":true,"description":"Belege für Kaffee","name":"Café ☕ Rechnungen","id":"cafe","examples":[]}]"#,
    );
    assert_eq!(categories::digest(true, &reordered), UNICODE_ON);
    let null_folder =
        parse(r#"[{"id":"x","name":"X","description":"d","catch_all":true,"folder":null}]"#);
    let no_folder = parse(r#"[{"id":"x","name":"X","description":"d","catch_all":true}]"#);
    assert_eq!(
        categories::digest(false, &null_folder),
        categories::digest(false, &no_folder)
    );
}

/// A config with the default `work` account, filing in `mode`.
fn config_with(dir: &Path, mode: FilingMode) -> PathBuf {
    let path = dir.join("mailtriage.json");
    let mut c = config::default_config();
    c.state_dir = dir.join("state");
    c.accounts.get_mut("work").unwrap().filing.mode = mode;
    config::save(&path, &c).unwrap();
    path
}

fn reason(error: anyhow::Error) -> (i32, Option<&'static str>) {
    let e = error.downcast_ref::<ServiceError>().unwrap();
    (e.code, e.kind.reason())
}

#[test]
fn export_and_validate_report_the_digest() {
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = config_with(dir.path(), FilingMode::Off);
    let mut service = Service::open(&path).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(service.categories("work").unwrap()["digest"], DEFAULT_OFF);
    let checked = service.validate_categories("work", defaults()).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(checked["digest"], DEFAULT_OFF);
    assert_eq!(
        checked["changes"],
        json!({"added":[],"removed":[],"renamed":[],"folders_changed":[],"edited":[],"reclassifies":false})
    );
}

fn run(dir: &Path, args: &[&str]) -> (i32, Value) {
    let out = Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_mailtriage"))
        .current_dir(dir)
        .args(args)
        .env_remove("MAILTRIAGE_CONFIG")
        .output()
        .unwrap();
    (
        out.status.code().unwrap(),
        serde_json::from_slice(&out.stdout).unwrap_or(Value::Null),
    )
}

#[test]
fn apply_checks_the_expected_digest_first() {
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = config_with(dir.path(), FilingMode::Off);
    let mut renamed = defaults();
    renamed[3].name = "News Digest".into();
    fs::write(
        dir.path().join("cats.json"),
        json!({ "categories": renamed }).to_string(),
    )
    .unwrap();
    let apply = |digest: &str| {
        run(
            dir.path(),
            &[
                "categories",
                "apply",
                "--account",
                "work",
                "--file",
                "cats.json",
                "--expect-digest",
                digest,
                "--json",
            ],
        )
    };
    // A stale digest: exit 5, nothing written.
    let before = fs::read(&path).unwrap();
    let (code, out) = apply(DEFAULT_ON);
    assert_eq!(code, 5);
    assert_eq!(
        out["error"],
        json!({"code":5,"message":"categories changed since export; export again","reason":"categories_changed"})
    );
    assert_eq!(fs::read(&path).unwrap(), before);
    // The current digest: applied, and the result carries the new one.
    let (code, out) = apply(DEFAULT_OFF);
    assert_eq!(code, 0, "{out}");
    assert_eq!(out["categories"][3]["name"], "News Digest");
    assert_ne!(out["digest"], DEFAULT_OFF);
}

#[test]
fn turning_filing_off_between_export_and_apply_is_a_change() {
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = config_with(dir.path(), FilingMode::DryRun);
    let digest = Service::open(&path).unwrap().categories("work").unwrap()["digest"]
        .as_str()
        .unwrap()
        .to_owned();
    assert_eq!(digest, categories::digest(true, &defaults()));
    let mut c: AppConfig = config::load(&path).unwrap();
    c.accounts.get_mut("work").unwrap().filing.mode = FilingMode::Off;
    config::save(&path, &c).unwrap();
    let error = Service::open(&path)
        .unwrap()
        .apply_categories_expecting("work", defaults(), Some(&digest))
        .unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(reason(error), (5, Some("categories_changed")));
}

/// The test hook for `config_changed` is the library: the file changes
/// between the command's read and its write.
#[test]
fn a_config_rewritten_during_apply_is_config_changed() {
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = config_with(dir.path(), FilingMode::Off);
    let mut service = Service::open(&path).unwrap();
    let mut c = config::load(&path).unwrap();
    c.accounts.get_mut("work").unwrap().brief = "changed".into();
    config::save(&path, &c).unwrap();
    let error = service
        .apply_categories_expecting("work", defaults(), Some(DEFAULT_OFF))
        .unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(reason(error), (5, ErrorKind::ConfigChanged.reason()));
}

fn changes_for(mode: FilingMode, edit: impl FnOnce(&mut Vec<Category>)) -> Changes {
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = config_with(dir.path(), mode);
    let service = Service::open(&path).unwrap();
    let mut next = defaults();
    edit(&mut next);
    let previous = &service.config.accounts["work"];
    categories::changes(
        &previous.categories,
        &categories::normalized(previous, next),
    )
}

#[test]
fn changes_name_each_kind() {
    let changes = changes_for(FilingMode::DryRun, |next| {
        next.retain(|c| c.id != "promotions");
        next[1].folder = Some("Receipts".into());
        next[2].description = "Status mail".into();
        next[3].name = "News Digest".into();
        next.push(Category {
            id: "travel".into(),
            name: "Travel".into(),
            description: "Trips".into(),
            examples: vec![],
            catch_all: false,
            folder: None,
        });
    });
    assert_eq!(
        serde_json::to_value(&changes).unwrap(),
        json!({
            "added": ["travel"],
            "removed": [{"id":"promotions","folder":"Promotions"}],
            "renamed": [{"id":"newsletters","from":"Newsletters","to":"News Digest"}],
            "folders_changed": [{"id":"transactions","from":"Transactions","to":"Receipts"}],
            "edited": ["updates"],
            "reclassifies": true
        })
    );
}

#[test]
fn a_rename_keeps_its_folder_only_with_filing_on() {
    let rename = |next: &mut Vec<Category>| next[3].name = "News Digest".into();
    let on = changes_for(FilingMode::DryRun, rename);
    assert_eq!(on.renamed.len(), 1);
    assert!(on.folders_changed.is_empty());
    assert!(!on.reclassifies);
    let off = changes_for(FilingMode::Off, rename);
    assert_eq!(off.folders_changed.len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(off.folders_changed[0].to, "News Digest");
}

type Edit = Box<dyn Fn(&mut Vec<Category>)>;

#[test]
fn reclassifies_matches_whether_apply_advances_the_revision() {
    let edits: Vec<(&str, Edit)> = vec![
        ("rename", Box::new(|n| n[3].name = "News Digest".into())),
        (
            "description",
            Box::new(|n| n[2].description = "Status mail".into()),
        ),
        (
            "examples",
            Box::new(|n| n[2].examples = vec!["Build passed".into()]),
        ),
        ("reorder", Box::new(|n| n.swap(0, 1))),
        (
            "folder",
            Box::new(|n| n[1].folder = Some("Receipts".into())),
        ),
        ("remove", Box::new(|n| n.retain(|c| c.id != "promotions"))),
    ];
    for (what, edit) in edits {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let path = config_with(dir.path(), FilingMode::DryRun);
        let mut next = defaults();
        edit(&mut next);
        let mut service = Service::open(&path).unwrap();
        let checked = service.validate_categories("work", next.clone()).unwrap();
        let before = service.config.accounts["work"].taxonomy_revision;
        let applied = service.apply_categories("work", next).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            checked["changes"]["reclassifies"],
            applied["taxonomy_revision"].as_u64().unwrap() > before,
            "{what}"
        );
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Add `src/categories.rs`**

```rust
//! What `categories apply` would write for an account: the digest that
//! identifies its categories and filing state, apply's folder normalization,
//! and the changes a category file makes.
use crate::domain::{AccountConfig, Category, FilingMode};
use serde::Serialize;
use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};

/// The digest input. Field order is part of the `v1` encoding.
#[derive(Serialize)]
struct DigestInput<'a> {
    filing_on: bool,
    categories: &'a [Category],
}

/// Whether the account's configured filing mode is not `off`. With filing
/// on, `apply` keeps the folder of an existing category without `folder`.
pub fn filing_on(account: &AccountConfig) -> bool {
    account.filing.mode != FilingMode::Off
}

/// `v1:` and the lowercase hex SHA-256 of the typed values, so key order and
/// `"folder": null` versus no `folder` in an input file do not matter, while
/// category order does.
pub fn digest(filing_on: bool, categories: &[Category]) -> String {
    let bytes = serde_json::to_vec(&DigestInput {
        filing_on,
        categories,
    })
    .expect("categories always serialize");
    format!("v1:{:x}", Sha256::digest(bytes))
}

/// The digest of an account's current categories and filing state.
pub fn account_digest(account: &AccountConfig) -> String {
    digest(filing_on(account), &account.categories)
}

/// `apply`'s normalization: with filing on, a category without `folder`
/// keeps the folder its ID had before (a rename does not move its folder),
/// and a new one uses its name.
pub fn normalized(previous: &AccountConfig, mut categories: Vec<Category>) -> Vec<Category> {
    if filing_on(previous) {
        for c in categories.iter_mut().filter(|c| c.folder.is_none()) {
            let folder = match previous.categories.iter().find(|p| p.id == c.id) {
                Some(before) => before.effective_folder().to_string(),
                None => c.name.clone(),
            };
            c.folder = Some(folder);
        }
    }
    categories
}

/// A removed category and the folder its mail was filed to.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
pub struct Removed {
    pub id: String,
    pub folder: String,
}

/// A value of a category that changed.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
pub struct Change {
    pub id: String,
    pub from: String,
    pub to: String,
}

/// `categories validate --account`'s `changes`. Folder entries hold
/// effective folder names, for display only.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
pub struct Changes {
    pub added: Vec<String>,
    pub removed: Vec<Removed>,
    pub renamed: Vec<Change>,
    pub folders_changed: Vec<Change>,
    pub edited: Vec<String>,
    pub reclassifies: bool,
}

/// What applying `next` (already normalized) changes in `previous`. Added,
/// renamed, re-pointed and edited categories are listed in `next`'s order,
/// removed ones in `previous`'s. `reclassifies` is exactly `apply`'s rule
/// for advancing `taxonomy_revision`.
pub fn changes(previous: &[Category], next: &[Category]) -> Changes {
    let before = |id: &str| previous.iter().find(|p| p.id == id);
    let mut changes = Changes {
        reclassifies: crate::service::category_semantics(previous)
            != crate::service::category_semantics(next),
        ..Changes::default()
    };
    for c in next {
        let Some(p) = before(&c.id) else {
            changes.added.push(c.id.clone());
            continue;
        };
        if p.name != c.name {
            changes.renamed.push(Change {
                id: c.id.clone(),
                from: p.name.clone(),
                to: c.name.clone(),
            });
        }
        if p.effective_folder() != c.effective_folder() {
            changes.folders_changed.push(Change {
                id: c.id.clone(),
                from: p.effective_folder().to_string(),
                to: c.effective_folder().to_string(),
            });
        }
        if p.description != c.description || p.examples != c.examples || p.catch_all != c.catch_all
        {
            changes.edited.push(c.id.clone());
        }
    }
    for p in previous {
        if !next.iter().any(|c| c.id == p.id) {
            changes.removed.push(Removed {
                id: p.id.clone(),
                folder: p.effective_folder().to_string(),
            });
        }
    }
    changes
}
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Document it**

- `docs/guide.md`: after the `reclassify` paragraph of "## Categories" and before "## Filing into folders", add:

  ````markdown
  ### Checking a change before you apply it

  ```sh
  mailtriage categories export --account work --json > categories.json
  # edit categories.json
  mailtriage categories validate --file categories.json --account work --json
  mailtriage categories apply --account work --file categories.json --expect-digest "$(jq -r .digest categories.json)" --json
  ```

  - `categories export` and `categories validate --account` report `digest`: `v1:` and a SHA-256 of the account's categories and whether its filing is on. It changes whenever something changes how `apply` would write the categories. The key order of a file and `"folder": null` versus no `folder` do not change it; the category order does.
  - `categories apply --expect-digest DIGEST` writes nothing and exits 5 with `categories changed since export; export again` (reason `categories_changed`) when the account's categories changed since the export (another window or agent applied a change), or filing was turned on or off. Export again and redo the edit.
  - `categories validate --file FILE --account NAME` also reports `changes`, computed after the same folder rules `apply` uses:
    - `added` lists category IDs;
    - `removed` lists `{"id","folder"}`;
    - `renamed` and `folders_changed` list `{"id","from","to"}`;
    - `edited` lists categories whose description, examples or default flag changed;
    - `reclassifies` is `true` exactly when `apply` would sort all open mail again (it advances `taxonomy_revision`, which uses your OpenRouter key).

    Folder names in `changes` are for display.
  ````

- `docs/guide.md`, Reference → Output and exit codes: append `categories_changed` (`categories apply --expect-digest` found other categories) to the list of reasons in the paragraph that starts "Some errors also carry a machine-readable `reason`".
- `docs/agents/index.md`: add a section before "## What is safe to repeat":

  ```markdown
  ## Editing categories

  Change categories only when the user asks. Export, edit, check, then apply with the digest from the export:

  1. `categories export --account work --json`; keep `digest`.
  2. `categories validate --file FILE --account work --json`. Exit 2 means the file breaks a rule; the message names it. Read `changes`, and tell the user before applying when `changes.reclassifies` is `true`: all open mail is classified again, which uses their OpenRouter key.
  3. `categories apply --account work --file FILE --expect-digest DIGEST --json`. Exit 5 with reason `categories_changed` means someone else changed the categories since your export: export again and redo the edit; do not loop.
  ```

```diff
diff --git a/tests/common/mod.rs b/tests/common/mod.rs
index d3c4968..1e72685 100644
--- a/tests/common/mod.rs
+++ b/tests/common/mod.rs
@@ -72,13 +72,94 @@ pub fn mail(message_id: &str, subject: &str, body: &str) -> Vec<u8> {
     format!("Message-ID: <{message_id}@test>\r\nFrom: Alex <alex@example.com>\r\nTo: work@example.com\r\nSubject: {subject}\r\nContent-Type: text/plain\r\n\r\n{body}\r\n").into_bytes()

-/// Fake `launchctl`: `bootstrap` loads, `bootout` unloads (exit 3 when not
-/// loaded), `print` describes a loaded job with tab-indented properties, as
-/// the real tool does. Calls go to `launchctl.log` next to the script.
-pub const LAUNCHCTL: &str = "#!/bin/sh\ndir=\"$(dirname \"$0\")\"\necho \"$*\" >> \"$dir/launchctl.log\"\ncase \"$1\" in\n  bootstrap) touch \"$dir/loaded\" ;;\n  bootout) [ -f \"$dir/loaded\" ] || exit 3; rm -f \"$dir/loaded\" ;;\n  print) [ -f \"$dir/loaded\" ] || exit 113; printf '%s = {\\n\\tstate = running\\n\\tpid = 4242\\n\\tlast exit code = 0\\n\\tendpoints = {\\n\\t\\tstate = active\\n\\t}\\n}\\n' \"$2\" ;;\n  *) exit 64 ;;\nesac\n";
+/// Fake `launchctl`, with its state in files next to the script:
+/// - `bootstrap` loads the plist and records its `ProgramArguments` in `args`
+///   (exit 5 with `bootstrap-fails`); the job runs unless `start-fails`
+///   exists, else it is loaded but idle;
+/// - `bootout` unloads (exit 3 when not loaded); `kickstart` runs a loaded job;
+/// - `print` describes a loaded job with tab-indented properties and its
+///   `arguments` block, as the real tool does (exit 113 when not loaded,
+///   1 with `print-fails`);
+/// - `enable`, `disable` and `print-disabled` keep the disabled labels in
+///   `disabled` (`print-disabled` exits 1 with `print-disabled-fails`).
+///
+/// Calls go to `launchctl.log` next to the script.
+pub const LAUNCHCTL: &str = r#"#!/bin/sh
+dir="$(dirname "$0")"
+echo "$*" >> "$dir/launchctl.log"
+label="${2##*/}"
+case "$1" in
+  bootstrap)
+    [ -f "$dir/bootstrap-fails" ] && exit 5
+    sed -n '/<key>ProgramArguments<\/key>/,/<\/array>/s/^ *<string>\(.*\)<\/string>$/\1/p' "$3" | sed 's/&lt;/</g; s/&gt;/>/g; s/&amp;/\&/g' > "$dir/args"
+    touch "$dir/loaded"
+    if [ -f "$dir/start-fails" ]; then touch "$dir/idle"; else rm -f "$dir/idle"; fi ;;
+  bootout) [ -f "$dir/loaded" ] || exit 3; rm -f "$dir/loaded" "$dir/args" "$dir/idle" ;;
+  kickstart) [ -f "$dir/loaded" ] || exit 113; if [ ! -f "$dir/start-fails" ]; then rm -f "$dir/idle"; fi ;;
+  print)
+    [ -f "$dir/print-fails" ] && exit 1
+    [ -f "$dir/loaded" ] || exit 113
+    printf '%s = {\n' "$2"
+    if [ -f "$dir/idle" ]; then printf '\tstate = waiting\n'; else printf '\tstate = running\n\tpid = 4242\n'; fi
+    printf '\tlast exit code = 0\n\targuments = {\n'
+    if [ -f "$dir/args" ]; then while IFS= read -r a; do printf '\t\t%s\n' "$a"; done < "$dir/args"; fi
+    printf '\t}\n\tendpoints = {\n\t\tstate = active\n\t}\n}\n' ;;
+  print-disabled)
+    [ -f "$dir/print-disabled-fails" ] && exit 1
+    printf '\tdisabled services = {\n\t\t"com.example.other" => disabled\n'
+    if [ -f "$dir/disabled" ]; then while IFS= read -r l; do printf '\t\t"%s" => disabled\n' "$l"; done < "$dir/disabled"; fi
+    printf '\t}\n' ;;
+  enable) if [ -f "$dir/disabled" ]; then grep -vxF "$label" "$dir/disabled" > "$dir/disabled.new"; mv "$dir/disabled.new" "$dir/disabled"; fi ;;
+  disable) echo "$label" >> "$dir/disabled" ;;
+  *) exit 64 ;;
+esac
+"#;
 
-/// Fake `systemctl --user`: `enable`, `restart`, `disable --now`, `show`.
-pub const SYSTEMCTL: &str = "#!/bin/sh\ndir=\"$(dirname \"$0\")\"\necho \"$*\" >> \"$dir/systemctl.log\"\ncase \"$2\" in\n  daemon-reload) ;;\n  enable) touch \"$dir/enabled\" ;;\n  restart) touch \"$dir/active\" ;;\n  disable) rm -f \"$dir/enabled\" \"$dir/active\" ;;\n  show) if [ -f \"$dir/active\" ]; then printf 'LoadState=loaded\\nActiveState=active\\nSubState=running\\nMainPID=4343\\nExecMainStatus=0\\n'; else printf 'LoadState=not-found\\nActiveState=inactive\\nSubState=dead\\nMainPID=0\\nExecMainStatus=0\\n'; fi ;;\n  *) exit 64 ;;\nesac\n";
+/// Fake `systemctl --user`, with its state in files next to the script:
+/// - `daemon-reload` records the `ExecStart=` of the unit file under
+///   `$MT_FAKE_HOME` (default: `home` next to the script) in `exec_start`,
+///   with `%%` and `$$` undone, as the loaded definition;
+/// - `enable [--now]`, `restart`, `disable [--now]` change `enabled` and
+///   `active` (`start-fails` keeps a start from running);
+/// - `is-enabled` prints `enabled`/`disabled`, or the text of `is-enabled`;
+///   `is-active` prints `active`/`inactive`;
+/// - `show` prints the requested properties; `MainPID` is `mainpid`'s text
+///   or 4343, `NeedDaemonReload` is `yes` with `needs-reload`, and it exits
+///   1 with `show-fails`.
+///
+/// Calls go to `systemctl.log` next to the script.
+pub const SYSTEMCTL: &str = r#"#!/bin/sh
+dir="$(dirname "$0")"
+echo "$*" >> "$dir/systemctl.log"
+home="${MT_FAKE_HOME:-$dir/home}"
+start() { if [ -f "$dir/start-fails" ]; then rm -f "$dir/active"; else touch "$dir/active"; fi; }
+case "$2" in
+  daemon-reload)
+    rm -f "$dir/exec_start" "$dir/needs-reload"
+    for f in "$home"/.config/systemd/user/mailtriage-*.service; do
+      if [ -f "$f" ]; then sed -n 's/^ExecStart=//p' "$f" | sed 's/%%/%/g; s/\$\$/$/g' > "$dir/exec_start"; fi
+    done ;;
+  enable) touch "$dir/enabled"; if [ "$3" = "--now" ]; then start; fi ;;
+  restart) start ;;
+  disable) rm -f "$dir/enabled"; if [ "$3" = "--now" ]; then rm -f "$dir/active"; fi ;;
+  is-enabled)
+    if [ -f "$dir/is-enabled" ]; then cat "$dir/is-enabled"; exit 1; fi
+    if [ -f "$dir/enabled" ]; then echo enabled; else echo disabled; exit 1; fi ;;
+  is-active) if [ -f "$dir/active" ]; then echo active; else echo inactive; exit 3; fi ;;
+  show)
+    [ -f "$dir/show-fails" ] && exit 1
+    if [ -f "$dir/active" ] || [ -f "$dir/exec_start" ]; then echo LoadState=loaded; else echo LoadState=not-found; fi
+    if [ -f "$dir/active" ]; then
+      printf 'ActiveState=active\nSubState=running\nMainPID=%s\n' "$(cat "$dir/mainpid" 2>/dev/null || echo 4343)"
+    else
+      printf 'ActiveState=inactive\nSubState=dead\nMainPID=0\n'
+    fi
+    echo ExecMainStatus=0
+    if [ -f "$dir/exec_start" ]; then printf 'ExecStart={ path=x ; argv[]=%s ; ignore_errors=no ; start_time=[n/a] ; stop_time=[n/a] ; pid=0 ; code=(null) ; status=0/0 }\n' "$(cat "$dir/exec_start")"; fi
+    if [ -f "$dir/needs-reload" ]; then echo NeedDaemonReload=yes; else echo NeedDaemonReload=no; fi ;;
+  *) exit 64 ;;
+esac
+"#;
 
 /// Writes an executable script.
 #[cfg(unix)]
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing tests**

Create `tests/service_status.rs`:

```rust
#![cfg(unix)]
//! `service status` for every account and the fields the tray reads: the
//! config a service runs with, whether it is enabled, its interval, filing
//! mode and identity. Fake `launchctl`/`systemctl` and a fake `/proc`.
mod common;
use common::{write_tool, LAUNCHCTL, SYSTEMCTL};
use mailtriage::{
    config,
    service_control::{self, Inspection},
    system_service::{self, Context, Manager, Unit},
};
use serde_json::{json, Value};
use std::{
    fs,
    path::{Path, PathBuf},
    process::Command,
};

fn context(manager: Manager, dir: &Path) -> Context {
    write_tool(dir, "launchctl", LAUNCHCTL);
    write_tool(dir, "systemctl", SYSTEMCTL);
    Context {
        manager,
        home: dir.join("home"),
        tool: dir.join(match manager {
            Manager::Launchd => "launchctl",
            Manager::Systemd => "systemctl",
        }),
        uid: 501,
    }
}

/// A config file named `name` in `dir` with the default `work` account.
fn config_file(dir: &Path, name: &str) -> PathBuf {
    let path = dir.join(name);
    let mut c = config::default_config();
    c.state_dir = dir.join(format!("{name}.state"));
    config::save(&path, &c).unwrap();
    fs::canonicalize(&path).unwrap()
}

fn unit_for(dir: &Path, config: &Path) -> Unit {
    Unit {
        account: "work".into(),
        exe: dir.join("mailtriage"),
        config: config.to_path_buf(),
        interval_seconds: 60,
        limit: 100,
        log_dir: dir.join("logs"),
        path_env: None,
    }
}

/// A fake `/proc/<pid>` whose command line is `unit`'s.
fn proc_for(dir: &Path, pid: u32, unit: &Unit) -> PathBuf {
    let root = dir.join("proc");
    let entry = root.join(pid.to_string());
    fs::create_dir_all(&entry).unwrap();
    let fields: Vec<String> = (4..=21).map(|n| n.to_string()).collect();
    fs::write(
        entry.join("stat"),
        format!("{pid} (mailtriage) S {} 9000 0 0\n", fields.join(" ")),
    )
    .unwrap();
    let mut cmdline = unit.arguments().join("\0").into_bytes();
    cmdline.push(0);
    fs::write(entry.join("cmdline"), cmdline).unwrap();
    root
}

fn inspect(ctx: &Context, proc_root: &Path) -> Inspection {
    service_control::inspect(ctx, "work", proc_root)
}

fn matches(ctx: &Context, proc_root: &Path, config: &Path) -> Option<bool> {
    service_control::config_matches(&inspect(ctx, proc_root), ctx.manager, config)
}

#[test]
fn two_configs_that_share_an_account_name() {
    for manager in [Manager::Launchd, Manager::Systemd] {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let ctx = context(manager, dir.path());
        let (a, b) = (
            config_file(dir.path(), "a.json"),
            config_file(dir.path(), "b.json"),
        );
        let unit = unit_for(dir.path(), &a);
        system_service::install(&ctx, &unit).unwrap();
        let proc_root = proc_for(dir.path(), 4343, &unit);
        let found = inspect(&ctx, &proc_root);
        assert_eq!(
            found.service_config.as_deref(),
            Some(a.as_path()),
            "{manager:?}"
        );
        assert_eq!(found.file_config.as_deref(), Some(a.as_path()));
        assert_eq!(found.interval_seconds, Some(60));
        assert_eq!(matches(&ctx, &proc_root, &a), Some(true), "{manager:?}");
        assert_eq!(matches(&ctx, &proc_root, &b), Some(false), "{manager:?}");
        assert_eq!(
            service_control::other_config(&found, &b).as_deref(),
            Some(a.as_path())
        );
    }
}

/// An install for config B whose reload failed: the file names B, the
/// loaded job still runs A.
#[test]
fn a_unit_rewritten_for_another_config_while_the_old_job_runs() {
    for manager in [Manager::Launchd, Manager::Systemd] {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let ctx = context(manager, dir.path());
        let (a, b) = (
            config_file(dir.path(), "a.json"),
            config_file(dir.path(), "b.json"),
        );
        let unit_a = unit_for(dir.path(), &a);
        system_service::install(&ctx, &unit_a).unwrap();
        let proc_root = proc_for(dir.path(), 4343, &unit_a);
        let unit_b = unit_for(dir.path(), &b);
        let text = match manager {
            Manager::Launchd => system_service::plist(&unit_b),
            Manager::Systemd => system_service::systemd_unit(&unit_b),
        };
        fs::write(ctx.unit_path("work"), text).unwrap();
        if manager == Manager::Systemd {
            fs::write(dir.path().join("needs-reload"), "").unwrap();
        }
        assert_eq!(matches(&ctx, &proc_root, &a), Some(false), "{manager:?}");
        assert_eq!(matches(&ctx, &proc_root, &b), Some(false), "{manager:?}");
    }
}

/// systemd: a reload succeeded while the old process keeps running config A.
#[test]
fn systemd_compares_the_running_process_too() {
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let ctx = context(Manager::Systemd, dir.path());
    let (a, b) = (
        config_file(dir.path(), "a.json"),
        config_file(dir.path(), "b.json"),
    );
    let unit_a = unit_for(dir.path(), &a);
    system_service::install(&ctx, &unit_a).unwrap();
    let proc_root = proc_for(dir.path(), 4343, &unit_a);
    fs::write(
        ctx.unit_path("work"),
        system_service::systemd_unit(&unit_for(dir.path(), &b)),
    )
    .unwrap();
    let reload = Command::new(&ctx.tool)
        .args(["--user", "daemon-reload"])
        .status()
        .unwrap();
    assert!(reload.success());
    let found = inspect(&ctx, &proc_root);
    assert_eq!(found.service_config.as_deref(), Some(a.as_path()));
    assert_eq!(found.file_config.as_deref(), Some(b.as_path()));
    assert_eq!(matches(&ctx, &proc_root, &b), Some(false));
    // The process is gone from /proc: its config cannot be read.
    fs::write(dir.path().join("mainpid"), "4444").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(matches(&ctx, &proc_root, &b), None);
}

#[test]
fn failed_queries_and_pending_reloads_are_unknown() {
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let ctx = context(Manager::Launchd, dir.path());
    let a = config_file(dir.path(), "a.json");
    system_service::install(&ctx, &unit_for(dir.path(), &a)).unwrap();
    fs::write(dir.path().join("print-fails"), "").unwrap();
    let found = inspect(&ctx, Path::new("/nonexistent"));
    assert_eq!(found.service_config, None);
    assert_eq!(found.loaded, None);
    assert_eq!(matches(&ctx, Path::new("/nonexistent"), &a), None);

    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let ctx = context(Manager::Systemd, dir.path());
    let a = config_file(dir.path(), "a.json");
    let unit = unit_for(dir.path(), &a);
    system_service::install(&ctx, &unit).unwrap();
    let proc_root = proc_for(dir.path(), 4343, &unit);
    assert_eq!(matches(&ctx, &proc_root, &a), Some(true));
    fs::write(dir.path().join("needs-reload"), "").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(inspect(&ctx, &proc_root).needs_daemon_reload, Some(true));
    assert_eq!(matches(&ctx, &proc_root, &a), None);
    fs::remove_file(dir.path().join("needs-reload")).unwrap();
    fs::write(dir.path().join("show-fails"), "").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(matches(&ctx, &proc_root, &a), None);
}

#[test]
fn not_installed_matches_and_is_not_enabled() {
    for manager in [Manager::Launchd, Manager::Systemd] {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let ctx = context(manager, dir.path());
        let a = config_file(dir.path(), "a.json");
        let found = inspect(&ctx, Path::new("/nonexistent"));
        assert!(!found.installed);
        assert_eq!(
            (found.enabled, found.enablement.as_str()),
            (Some(false), "not_installed")
        );
        assert_eq!(matches(&ctx, Path::new("/nonexistent"), &a), Some(true));
    }
}

#[test]
fn enabled_and_enablement_for_each_state() {
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let ctx = context(Manager::Launchd, dir.path());
    let a = config_file(dir.path(), "a.json");
    system_service::install(&ctx, &unit_for(dir.path(), &a)).unwrap();
    let state = |ctx: &Context| {
        let found = inspect(ctx, Path::new("/nonexistent"));
        (found.enabled, found.enablement)
    };
    assert_eq!(state(&ctx), (Some(true), "enabled".into()));
    fs::write(
        dir.path().join("disabled"),
        "digital.wirdrei.mailtriage.work\n",
    )
    .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(state(&ctx), (Some(false), "disabled".into()));
    fs::write(dir.path().join("print-disabled-fails"), "").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(state(&ctx), (None, "unknown".into()));

    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let ctx = context(Manager::Systemd, dir.path());
    system_service::install(&ctx, &unit_for(dir.path(), &a)).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(state(&ctx), (Some(true), "enabled".into()));
    for (text, enabled) in [("masked", Some(false)), ("static", None)] {
        fs::write(dir.path().join("is-enabled"), format!("{text}\n")).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(state(&ctx), (enabled, text.to_owned()));
    }
}

fn run(dir: &Path, bin: &Path, args: &[&str]) -> (i32, Value) {
    let out = Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_mailtriage"))
        .current_dir(dir.join("cwd"))
        .args(args)
        .env("HOME", dir.join("home"))
        .env("MT_FAKE_HOME", dir.join("home"))
        .env("PATH", format!("{}:/usr/bin:/bin", bin.display()))
        .env_remove("MAILTRIAGE_CONFIG")
        .output()
        .unwrap();
    (
        out.status.code().unwrap(),
        serde_json::from_slice(&out.stdout).unwrap_or(Value::Null),
    )
}

#[test]
#[cfg(any(target_os = "macos", target_os = "linux"))]
fn status_reports_every_account_through_the_cli() {
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let bin = dir.path().join("bin");
    for d in ["home", "bin", "cwd"] {
        fs::create_dir_all(dir.path().join(d)).unwrap();
    }
    write_tool(&bin, "launchctl", LAUNCHCTL);
    write_tool(&bin, "systemctl", SYSTEMCTL);
    let path = dir.path().join("cwd/mailtriage.json");
    let mut c = config::default_config();
    c.state_dir = PathBuf::from(".state");
    let mut alpha = c.accounts["work"].clone();
    alpha.identity = "alpha@example.invalid".into();
    alpha.filing.mode = mailtriage::domain::FilingMode::DryRun;
    c.accounts.insert("alpha".into(), alpha);
    config::save(&path, &c).unwrap();
    let config = fs::canonicalize(&path).unwrap();

    let (code, v) = run(dir.path(), &bin, &["service", "status", "--json"]);
    assert_eq!(code, 0, "{v}");
    assert_eq!(v["config"], json!(config));
    let services = v["services"].as_array().unwrap();
    assert_eq!(services.len(), 2);
    assert_eq!(services[0]["account"], "alpha");
    assert_eq!(services[0]["filing_mode"], "dry_run");
    assert_eq!(services[0]["identity"], "alpha@example.invalid");
    assert_eq!(services[1]["account"], "work");
    assert_eq!(services[1]["filing_mode"], "off");
    for s in services {
        assert_eq!(s["installed"], false);
        assert_eq!(s["config_matches"], true);
        assert_eq!(s["enabled"], false);
        assert_eq!(s["enablement"], "not_installed");
        assert_eq!(s["service_config"], Value::Null);
        assert_eq!(s["file_config"], Value::Null);
        assert_eq!(s["interval_seconds"], Value::Null);
    }

    let (code, v) = run(
        dir.path(),
        &bin,
        &[
            "service",
            "install",
            "--account",
            "work",
            "--interval-seconds",
            "120",
            "--json",
        ],
    );
    assert_eq!(code, 0, "{v}");
    // On Linux the fake `systemctl` reports this process as the service's main PID.
    let mut watch = None;
    if cfg!(target_os = "linux") {
        write_tool(&bin, "fake-watch", "#!/bin/sh\nsleep 30\n");
        let child = Command::new(bin.join("fake-watch"))
            .args(["watch", "--config"])
            .arg(&config)
            .args([
                "--account",
                "work",
                "--interval-seconds",
                "120",
                "--limit",
                "100",
                "--json",
            ])
            .spawn()
            .unwrap();
        fs::write(bin.join("mainpid"), child.id().to_string()).unwrap();
        watch = Some(child);
    }
    let (code, v) = run(
        dir.path(),
        &bin,
        &["service", "status", "--account", "work", "--json"],
    );
    if let Some(mut child) = watch {
        child.kill().unwrap();
        child.wait().unwrap();
    }
    assert_eq!(code, 0, "{v}");
    assert_eq!(v["config"], json!(config));
    let s = &v["service"];
    assert_eq!(s["running"], true);
    assert_eq!(s["service_config"], json!(config));
    assert_eq!(s["file_config"], json!(config));
    assert_eq!(s["config_matches"], true, "{s}");
    assert_eq!(s["enabled"], true);
    assert_eq!(s["enablement"], "enabled");
    assert_eq!(s["interval_seconds"], 120);
    assert_eq!(
        s.get("needs_daemon_reload").is_some(),
        cfg!(target_os = "linux")
    );
}
```
